## SAGF Compliance Scan: `DVWA-master/login.php`
**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:39:27.206036+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (3)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) | No Hardcoded Secrets | 39 |
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) | Secure Password Hashing | 39 |
| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 27 |

#### [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) — Potential hardcoded secret detected
```php
// Line 39
$query  = "SELECT * FROM `users` WHERE user='$user' AND password='$pass';";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: store secrets in environment variables, never in code
import os
API_KEY = os.environ['API_KEY']        # Python
# const apiKey = process.env.API_KEY   # Node.js
# $apiKey = getenv('API_KEY');          # PHP
# Use Azure Key Vault, HashiCorp Vault, or AWS Secrets Manager in production.
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Hardcoding secrets is one of the most common ways attackers gain unauthorized access to cloud resources. This control eliminates credential exposure in the repository.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remove the secret from code, invalidate the token, and store it in an approved secret vault (e.g., Azure Key Vault or HashiCorp Vault).
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001)

#### [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) — Hardcoded password literal detected
```php
// Line 39
$query  = "SELECT * FROM `users` WHERE user='$user' AND password='$pass';";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: load passwords from environment, hash with bcrypt
import os, bcrypt
# Never store plain-text passwords
password_hash = bcrypt.hashpw(os.environ['USER_PASSWORD'].encode(), bcrypt.gensalt())
# Verify: bcrypt.checkpw(supplied_password.encode(), password_hash)
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: If a password database is breached, using weak or unsalted hashes (like MD5 or SHA1) allows attackers to use 'rainbow tables' to crack millions of passwords in seconds. Strong KDFs make this computationally infeasible.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must refactor the authentication code to use a modern, approved library for password hashing (e.g., `spring-security-crypto`) and not use deprecated functions like `java.security.MessageDigest.getInstance("MD5")`.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001)

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 27
$pass = md5( $pass );
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

#### [SAST-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001) — Dangerous code execution function detected
```js
// Line 7
//eval("page" + id + " = window.open(URL, '" + id + "', 'toolbar=0,scrollbars=1,location=0,statusbar=0,menubar=0,resizable=1,width=800,height=300,left=540,top=250');");
```
**Suggested fix:**
```js
# Suggested fix: avoid eval/exec; use subprocess with a list (no shell=True)
import subprocess
# WRONG:  subprocess.call('ls ' + user_input, shell=True)
result = subprocess.run(['ls', user_input], capture_output=True, text=True)  # safe
# For dynamic config: use ast.literal_eval() or json.loads() instead of eval()
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: This is the primary defense against code-level flaws. It directly blocks the introduction of vulnerabilities like those found in penetration tests (e.g., Insecure File Upload, IDOR logic). It shifts discovery from a late-stage pen test to the d
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remediate the flagged vulnerability (e.g., use a parameterized query instead of string concatenation) to meet the quality gate standards.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001)

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 651

**Suggested fix:**

```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (1)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 18 |

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 18
$target_file   =  md5( uniqid() . $uploaded_name ) . '.' . $uploaded_ext;
// Line 20
$temp_file    .= DIRECTORY_SEPARATOR . md5( uniqid() . $uploaded_name ) . '.' . $uploaded_ext;
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

## SAGF Compliance Scan: `DVWA-master/vulnerabilities/weak_id/source/high.php`
**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:39:31.560168+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (1)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 10 |

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 10
$cookie_value = md5($_SESSION['last_session_id_high']);
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

## SAGF Compliance Scan: `DVWA-master/dvwa/includes/DBMS/MySQL.php`
**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:39:32.852849+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (1)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 55 |

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 55
('1','admin','admin','admin',MD5('password'),'{$avatarUrl}admin.jpg', NOW(), '0'),
// Line 56
('2','Gordon','Brown','gordonb',MD5('abc123'),'{$avatarUrl}gordonb.jpg', NOW(), '0'),
// Line 57
('3','Hack','Me','1337',MD5('charley'),'{$avatarUrl}1337.jpg', NOW(), '0'),
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

#### [SAST-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001) — Dangerous code execution function detected
```php
// Line 57
$response = curl_exec($ch);
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: avoid eval/exec; use subprocess with a list (no shell=True)
import subprocess
# WRONG:  subprocess.call('ls ' + user_input, shell=True)
result = subprocess.run(['ls', user_input], capture_output=True, text=True)  # safe
# For dynamic config: use ast.literal_eval() or json.loads() instead of eval()
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: This is the primary defense against code-level flaws. It directly blocks the introduction of vulnerabilities like those found in penetration tests (e.g., Insecure File Upload, IDOR logic). It shifts discovery from a late-stage pen test to the d
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remediate the flagged vulnerability (e.g., use a parameterized query instead of string concatenation) to meet the quality gate standards.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001)

#### [SAST-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001) — Dangerous code execution function detected
```php
// Line 20
eval( '?>' . file_get_contents( DVWA_WEB_PAGE_TO_ROOT . "vulnerabilities/{$id}/help/help.php" ) . '<?php ' );
// Line 22
eval( '?>' . file_get_contents( DVWA_WEB_PAGE_TO_ROOT . "vulnerabilities/{$id}/help/help.{$locale}.php" ) . '<?php ' );
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: avoid eval/exec; use subprocess with a list (no shell=True)
import subprocess
# WRONG:  subprocess.call('ls ' + user_input, shell=True)
result = subprocess.run(['ls', user_input], capture_output=True, text=True)  # safe
# For dynamic config: use ast.literal_eval() or json.loads() instead of eval()
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: This is the primary defense against code-level flaws. It directly blocks the introduction of vulnerabilities like those found in penetration tests (e.g., Insecure File Upload, IDOR logic). It shifts discovery from a late-stage pen test to the d
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remediate the flagged vulnerability (e.g., use a parameterized query instead of string concatenation) to meet the quality gate standards.

#### [SAST-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001) — Dangerous code execution function detected
```php
// Line 10
$cmd = shell_exec( 'ping  ' . $target );
// Line 14
$cmd = shell_exec( 'ping  -c 4 ' . $target );
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: avoid eval/exec; use subprocess with a list (no shell=True)
import subprocess
# WRONG:  subprocess.call('ls ' + user_input, shell=True)
result = subprocess.run(['ls', user_input], capture_output=True, text=True)  # safe
# For dynamic config: use ast.literal_eval() or json.loads() instead of eval()
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: This is the primary defense against code-level flaws. It directly blocks the introduction of vulnerabilities like those found in penetration tests (e.g., Insecure File Upload, IDOR logic). It shifts discovery from a late-stage pen test to the d
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remediate the flagged vulnerability (e.g., use a parameterized query instead of string concatenation) to meet the quality gate standards.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001)

#### [SAST-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001) — Dangerous code execution function detected
```php
// Line 26
$cmd = shell_exec( 'ping  ' . $target );
// Line 30
$cmd = shell_exec( 'ping  -c 4 ' . $target );
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: avoid eval/exec; use subprocess with a list (no shell=True)
import subprocess
# WRONG:  subprocess.call('ls ' + user_input, shell=True)
result = subprocess.run(['ls', user_input], capture_output=True, text=True)  # safe
# For dynamic config: use ast.literal_eval() or json.loads() instead of eval()
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: This is the primary defense against code-level flaws. It directly blocks the introduction of vulnerabilities like those found in penetration tests (e.g., Insecure File Upload, IDOR logic). It shifts discovery from a late-stage pen test to the d
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remediate the flagged vulnerability (e.g., use a parameterized query instead of string concatenation) to meet the quality gate standards.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001)

## SAGF Compliance Scan: `DVWA-master/vulnerabilities/captcha/source/medium.php`
**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:39:39.435145+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (3)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) | No Hardcoded Secrets | 68 |
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) | Secure Password Hashing | 68 |
| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 65 |

#### [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) — Potential hardcoded secret detected
```php
// Line 68
$insert = "UPDATE `users` SET password = '$pass_new' WHERE user = '" . dvwaCurrentUser() . "';";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: store secrets in environment variables, never in code
import os
API_KEY = os.environ['API_KEY']        # Python
# const apiKey = process.env.API_KEY   # Node.js
# $apiKey = getenv('API_KEY');          # PHP
# Use Azure Key Vault, HashiCorp Vault, or AWS Secrets Manager in production.
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Hardcoding secrets is one of the most common ways attackers gain unauthorized access to cloud resources. This control eliminates credential exposure in the repository.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remove the secret from code, invalidate the token, and store it in an approved secret vault (e.g., Azure Key Vault or HashiCorp Vault).
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001)

#### [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) — Hardcoded password literal detected
```php
// Line 68
$insert = "UPDATE `users` SET password = '$pass_new' WHERE user = '" . dvwaCurrentUser() . "';";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: load passwords from environment, hash with bcrypt
import os, bcrypt
# Never store plain-text passwords
password_hash = bcrypt.hashpw(os.environ['USER_PASSWORD'].encode(), bcrypt.gensalt())
# Verify: bcrypt.checkpw(supplied_password.encode(), password_hash)
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: If a password database is breached, using weak or unsalted hashes (like MD5 or SHA1) allows attackers to use 'rainbow tables' to crack millions of passwords in seconds. Strong KDFs make this computationally infeasible.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must refactor the authentication code to use a modern, approved library for password hashing (e.g., `spring-security-crypto`) and not use deprecated functions like `java.security.MessageDigest.getInstance("MD5")`.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001)

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 65
$pass_new = md5( $pass_new );
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

## SAGF Compliance Scan: `DVWA-master/vulnerabilities/captcha/source/low.php`
**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:39:39.849075+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (3)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) | No Hardcoded Secrets | 60 |
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) | Secure Password Hashing | 60 |
| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 57 |

#### [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) — Potential hardcoded secret detected
```php
// Line 60
$insert = "UPDATE `users` SET password = '$pass_new' WHERE user = '" . dvwaCurrentUser() . "';";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: store secrets in environment variables, never in code
import os
API_KEY = os.environ['API_KEY']        # Python
# const apiKey = process.env.API_KEY   # Node.js
# $apiKey = getenv('API_KEY');          # PHP
# Use Azure Key Vault, HashiCorp Vault, or AWS Secrets Manager in production.
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Hardcoding secrets is one of the most common ways attackers gain unauthorized access to cloud resources. This control eliminates credential exposure in the repository.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remove the secret from code, invalidate the token, and store it in an approved secret vault (e.g., Azure Key Vault or HashiCorp Vault).
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001)

#### [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) — Hardcoded password literal detected
```php
// Line 60
$insert = "UPDATE `users` SET password = '$pass_new' WHERE user = '" . dvwaCurrentUser() . "';";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: load passwords from environment, hash with bcrypt
import os, bcrypt
# Never store plain-text passwords
password_hash = bcrypt.hashpw(os.environ['USER_PASSWORD'].encode(), bcrypt.gensalt())
# Verify: bcrypt.checkpw(supplied_password.encode(), password_hash)
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: If a password database is breached, using weak or unsalted hashes (like MD5 or SHA1) allows attackers to use 'rainbow tables' to crack millions of passwords in seconds. Strong KDFs make this computationally infeasible.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must refactor the authentication code to use a modern, approved library for password hashing (e.g., `spring-security-crypto`) and not use deprecated functions like `java.security.MessageDigest.getInstance("MD5")`.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001)

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 57
$pass_new = md5( $pass_new );
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

## SAGF Compliance Scan: `DVWA-master/vulnerabilities/captcha/source/high.php`
**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:39:39.991205+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (3)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) | No Hardcoded Secrets | 30 |
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) | Secure Password Hashing | 30 |
| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 27 |

#### [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) — Potential hardcoded secret detected
```php
// Line 30
$insert = "UPDATE `users` SET password = '$pass_new' WHERE user = '" . dvwaCurrentUser() . "' LIMIT 1;";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: store secrets in environment variables, never in code
import os
API_KEY = os.environ['API_KEY']        # Python
# const apiKey = process.env.API_KEY   # Node.js
# $apiKey = getenv('API_KEY');          # PHP
# Use Azure Key Vault, HashiCorp Vault, or AWS Secrets Manager in production.
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Hardcoding secrets is one of the most common ways attackers gain unauthorized access to cloud resources. This control eliminates credential exposure in the repository.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remove the secret from code, invalidate the token, and store it in an approved secret vault (e.g., Azure Key Vault or HashiCorp Vault).
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001)

#### [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) — Hardcoded password literal detected
```php
// Line 30
$insert = "UPDATE `users` SET password = '$pass_new' WHERE user = '" . dvwaCurrentUser() . "' LIMIT 1;";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: load passwords from environment, hash with bcrypt
import os, bcrypt
# Never store plain-text passwords
password_hash = bcrypt.hashpw(os.environ['USER_PASSWORD'].encode(), bcrypt.gensalt())
# Verify: bcrypt.checkpw(supplied_password.encode(), password_hash)
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: If a password database is breached, using weak or unsalted hashes (like MD5 or SHA1) allows attackers to use 'rainbow tables' to crack millions of passwords in seconds. Strong KDFs make this computationally infeasible.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must refactor the authentication code to use a modern, approved library for password hashing (e.g., `spring-security-crypto`) and not use deprecated functions like `java.security.MessageDigest.getInstance("MD5")`.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001)

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 27
$pass_new = md5( $pass_new );
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |

#### [SAST-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001) — Dangerous code execution function detected
```php
// Line 88
exec ("ping -c 4 " . $target, $output, $ret_var);
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: avoid eval/exec; use subprocess with a list (no shell=True)
import subprocess
# WRONG:  subprocess.call('ls ' + user_input, shell=True)
result = subprocess.run(['ls', user_input], capture_output=True, text=True)  # safe
# For dynamic config: use ast.literal_eval() or json.loads() instead of eval()
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: This is the primary defense against code-level flaws. It directly blocks the introduction of vulnerabilities like those found in penetration tests (e.g., Insecure File Upload, IDOR logic). It shifts discovery from a late-stage pen test to the d
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remediate the flagged vulnerability (e.g., use a parameterized query instead of string concatenation) to meet the quality gate standards.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001)

## SAGF Compliance Scan: `DVWA-master/vulnerabilities/sqli/test.php`
**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:39:42.774316+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (2)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) | No Hardcoded Secrets | 4 |
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) | Secure Password Hashing | 4 |

#### [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) — Potential hardcoded secret detected
```php
// Line 4
$password = "password";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: store secrets in environment variables, never in code
import os
API_KEY = os.environ['API_KEY']        # Python
# const apiKey = process.env.API_KEY   # Node.js
# $apiKey = getenv('API_KEY');          # PHP
# Use Azure Key Vault, HashiCorp Vault, or AWS Secrets Manager in production.
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Hardcoding secrets is one of the most common ways attackers gain unauthorized access to cloud resources. This control eliminates credential exposure in the repository.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remove the secret from code, invalidate the token, and store it in an approved secret vault (e.g., Azure Key Vault or HashiCorp Vault).
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001)

#### [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) — Hardcoded password literal detected
```php
// Line 4
$password = "password";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: load passwords from environment, hash with bcrypt
import os, bcrypt
# Never store plain-text passwords
password_hash = bcrypt.hashpw(os.environ['USER_PASSWORD'].encode(), bcrypt.gensalt())
# Verify: bcrypt.checkpw(supplied_password.encode(), password_hash)
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: If a password database is breached, using weak or unsalted hashes (like MD5 or SHA1) allows attackers to use 'rainbow tables' to crack millions of passwords in seconds. Strong KDFs make this computationally infeasible.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must refactor the authentication code to use a modern, approved library for password hashing (e.g., `spring-security-crypto`) and not use deprecated functions like `java.security.MessageDigest.getInstance("MD5")`.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001)

## SAGF Compliance Scan: `DVWA-master/vulnerabilities/brute/source/medium.php`
**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:39:44.176482+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (3)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) | No Hardcoded Secrets | 14 |
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) | Secure Password Hashing | 14 |
| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 11 |

#### [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) — Potential hardcoded secret detected
```php
// Line 14
$query  = "SELECT * FROM `users` WHERE user = '$user' AND password = '$pass';";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: store secrets in environment variables, never in code
import os
API_KEY = os.environ['API_KEY']        # Python
# const apiKey = process.env.API_KEY   # Node.js
# $apiKey = getenv('API_KEY');          # PHP
# Use Azure Key Vault, HashiCorp Vault, or AWS Secrets Manager in production.
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Hardcoding secrets is one of the most common ways attackers gain unauthorized access to cloud resources. This control eliminates credential exposure in the repository.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remove the secret from code, invalidate the token, and store it in an approved secret vault (e.g., Azure Key Vault or HashiCorp Vault).
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001)

#### [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) — Hardcoded password literal detected
```php
// Line 14
$query  = "SELECT * FROM `users` WHERE user = '$user' AND password = '$pass';";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: load passwords from environment, hash with bcrypt
import os, bcrypt
# Never store plain-text passwords
password_hash = bcrypt.hashpw(os.environ['USER_PASSWORD'].encode(), bcrypt.gensalt())
# Verify: bcrypt.checkpw(supplied_password.encode(), password_hash)
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: If a password database is breached, using weak or unsalted hashes (like MD5 or SHA1) allows attackers to use 'rainbow tables' to crack millions of passwords in seconds. Strong KDFs make this computationally infeasible.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must refactor the authentication code to use a modern, approved library for password hashing (e.g., `spring-security-crypto`) and not use deprecated functions like `java.security.MessageDigest.getInstance("MD5")`.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001)

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 11
$pass = md5( $pass );
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:39:51.932510+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (1)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 16 |

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 16
$pass = md5( $pass );
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (3)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) | No Hardcoded Secrets | 19 |
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) | Secure Password Hashing | 19 |
| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 16 |

#### [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) — Potential hardcoded secret detected
```php
// Line 19
$query  = "SELECT * FROM `users` WHERE user = '$user' AND password = '$pass';";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: store secrets in environment variables, never in code
import os
API_KEY = os.environ['API_KEY']        # Python
# const apiKey = process.env.API_KEY   # Node.js
# $apiKey = getenv('API_KEY');          # PHP
# Use Azure Key Vault, HashiCorp Vault, or AWS Secrets Manager in production.
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Hardcoding secrets is one of the most common ways attackers gain unauthorized access to cloud resources. This control eliminates credential exposure in the repository.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remove the secret from code, invalidate the token, and store it in an approved secret vault (e.g., Azure Key Vault or HashiCorp Vault).
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001)

#### [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) — Hardcoded password literal detected
```php
// Line 19
$query  = "SELECT * FROM `users` WHERE user = '$user' AND password = '$pass';";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: load passwords from environment, hash with bcrypt
import os, bcrypt
# Never store plain-text passwords
password_hash = bcrypt.hashpw(os.environ['USER_PASSWORD'].encode(), bcrypt.gensalt())
# Verify: bcrypt.checkpw(supplied_password.encode(), password_hash)
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: If a password database is breached, using weak or unsalted hashes (like MD5 or SHA1) allows attackers to use 'rainbow tables' to crack millions of passwords in seconds. Strong KDFs make this computationally infeasible.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must refactor the authentication code to use a modern, approved library for password hashing (e.g., `spring-security-crypto`) and not use deprecated functions like `java.security.MessageDigest.getInstance("MD5")`.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001)

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 16
$pass = md5( $pass );
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

#### [SAST-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001) — Dangerous code execution function detected
```php
// Line 51
$git_log = shell_exec ("git -c 'safe.directory=*' log -1");
// Line 59
$out = shell_exec ("apachectl -M | grep rewrite_module");
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: avoid eval/exec; use subprocess with a list (no shell=True)
import subprocess
# WRONG:  subprocess.call('ls ' + user_input, shell=True)
result = subprocess.run(['ls', user_input], capture_output=True, text=True)  # safe
# For dynamic config: use ast.literal_eval() or json.loads() instead of eval()
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: This is the primary defense against code-level flaws. It directly blocks the introduction of vulnerabilities like those found in penetration tests (e.g., Insecure File Upload, IDOR logic). It shifts discovery from a late-stage pen test to the d
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remediate the flagged vulnerability (e.g., use a parameterized query instead of string concatenation) to meet the quality gate standards.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001)

**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:39:51.237741+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (1)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🟠 HIGH | [CORS-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CORS-M-001) | No Reflected Origin (CORS) | 11 |

#### [CORS-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CORS-M-001) — Wildcard CORS origin (*) allows any domain
```php
// Line 11
header("Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *");
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
// Suggested fix: restrict CORS to an explicit allowlist
const cors = require('cors');
const allowed = ['https://app.example.com', 'https://admin.example.com'];
app.use(cors({
    origin: (origin, cb) => allowed.includes(origin) ? cb(null, true) : cb(new Error('Not allowed')),
    credentials: true
}));
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: A reflective CORS policy allows *any* malicious site to make authenticated requests from a logged-in user's browser, bypassing the Same-Origin Policy and stealing their data. This was a critical finding.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must configure the API backend to use a strict whitelist of allowed domains for CORS, and *never* reflect the `Origin` header from the request.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CORS-M-001)

#### [SAST-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001) — Dangerous code execution function detected
```php
// Line 39
$out = shell_exec ("apachectl -M | grep rewrite_module");
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: avoid eval/exec; use subprocess with a list (no shell=True)
import subprocess
# WRONG:  subprocess.call('ls ' + user_input, shell=True)
result = subprocess.run(['ls', user_input], capture_output=True, text=True)  # safe
# For dynamic config: use ast.literal_eval() or json.loads() instead of eval()
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: This is the primary defense against code-level flaws. It directly blocks the introduction of vulnerabilities like those found in penetration tests (e.g., Insecure File Upload, IDOR logic). It shifts discovery from a late-stage pen test to the d
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remediate the flagged vulnerability (e.g., use a parameterized query instead of string concatenation) to meet the quality gate standards.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001)

## SAGF Compliance Scan: `DVWA-master/vulnerabilities/csrf/test_credentials.php`
**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:39:52.652584+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (3)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) | No Hardcoded Secrets | 21 |
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) | Secure Password Hashing | 21 |
| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 19 |

#### [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) — Potential hardcoded secret detected
```php
// Line 21
$query  = "SELECT * FROM `users` WHERE user='$user' AND password='$pass';";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: store secrets in environment variables, never in code
import os
API_KEY = os.environ['API_KEY']        # Python
# const apiKey = process.env.API_KEY   # Node.js
# $apiKey = getenv('API_KEY');          # PHP
# Use Azure Key Vault, HashiCorp Vault, or AWS Secrets Manager in production.
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Hardcoding secrets is one of the most common ways attackers gain unauthorized access to cloud resources. This control eliminates credential exposure in the repository.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remove the secret from code, invalidate the token, and store it in an approved secret vault (e.g., Azure Key Vault or HashiCorp Vault).
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001)

#### [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) — Hardcoded password literal detected
```php
// Line 21
$query  = "SELECT * FROM `users` WHERE user='$user' AND password='$pass';";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: load passwords from environment, hash with bcrypt
import os, bcrypt
# Never store plain-text passwords
password_hash = bcrypt.hashpw(os.environ['USER_PASSWORD'].encode(), bcrypt.gensalt())
# Verify: bcrypt.checkpw(supplied_password.encode(), password_hash)
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: If a password database is breached, using weak or unsalted hashes (like MD5 or SHA1) allows attackers to use 'rainbow tables' to crack millions of passwords in seconds. Strong KDFs make this computationally infeasible.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must refactor the authentication code to use a modern, approved library for password hashing (e.g., `spring-security-crypto`) and not use deprecated functions like `java.security.MessageDigest.getInstance("MD5")`.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001)

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 19
$pass = md5( $pass );
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

## SAGF Compliance Scan: `DVWA-master/vulnerabilities/api/gen_openapi.php`
**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:39:51.100648+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (1)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🟠 HIGH | [CORS-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CORS-M-001) | No Reflected Origin (CORS) | 6 |

#### [CORS-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CORS-M-001) — Wildcard CORS origin (*) allows any domain
```php
// Line 6
header("Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *");
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
// Suggested fix: restrict CORS to an explicit allowlist
const cors = require('cors');
const allowed = ['https://app.example.com', 'https://admin.example.com'];
app.use(cors({
    origin: (origin, cb) => allowed.includes(origin) ? cb(null, true) : cb(new Error('Not allowed')),
    credentials: true
}));
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: A reflective CORS policy allows *any* malicious site to make authenticated requests from a logged-in user's browser, bypassing the Same-Origin Policy and stealing their data. This was a critical finding.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must configure the API backend to use a strict whitelist of allowed domains for CORS, and *never* reflect the `Origin` header from the request.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CORS-M-001)

## SAGF Compliance Scan: `DVWA-master/vulnerabilities/csrf/source/impossible.php`
**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:39:58.885930+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (1)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 15 |

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 15
$pass_curr = md5( $pass_curr );
// Line 29
$pass_new = md5( $pass_new );
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

## SAGF Compliance Scan: `DVWA-master/vulnerabilities/csrf/source/low.php`
**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:39:59.033052+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (3)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) | No Hardcoded Secrets | 16 |
| 🔴 CRITICAL | [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) | Secure Password Hashing | 16 |
| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 12 |

#### [SECR-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001) — Potential hardcoded secret detected
```php
// Line 16
$insert = "UPDATE `users` SET password = '$pass_new' WHERE user = '" . $current_user . "';";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: store secrets in environment variables, never in code
import os
API_KEY = os.environ['API_KEY']        # Python
# const apiKey = process.env.API_KEY   # Node.js
# $apiKey = getenv('API_KEY');          # PHP
# Use Azure Key Vault, HashiCorp Vault, or AWS Secrets Manager in production.
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Hardcoding secrets is one of the most common ways attackers gain unauthorized access to cloud resources. This control eliminates credential exposure in the repository.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remove the secret from code, invalidate the token, and store it in an approved secret vault (e.g., Azure Key Vault or HashiCorp Vault).
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SECR-M-001)

#### [AUTH-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001) — Hardcoded password literal detected
```php
// Line 16
$insert = "UPDATE `users` SET password = '$pass_new' WHERE user = '" . $current_user . "';";
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: load passwords from environment, hash with bcrypt
import os, bcrypt
# Never store plain-text passwords
password_hash = bcrypt.hashpw(os.environ['USER_PASSWORD'].encode(), bcrypt.gensalt())
# Verify: bcrypt.checkpw(supplied_password.encode(), password_hash)
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: If a password database is breached, using weak or unsalted hashes (like MD5 or SHA1) allows attackers to use 'rainbow tables' to crack millions of passwords in seconds. Strong KDFs make this computationally infeasible.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must refactor the authentication code to use a modern, approved library for password hashing (e.g., `spring-security-crypto`) and not use deprecated functions like `java.security.MessageDigest.getInstance("MD5")`.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=AUTH-M-001)

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 12
$pass_new = md5( $pass_new );
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

**Scanned at**: 2026-10-08T05:40:00.868981+00:00
**Mandard DB coverage**: 93 applicable mandards checked (SECR, AUTH, CRYPT, SAST, HDR, ERR…)
**Attestation**: `eyJhbGciOiAiSFMyNTYiLCAidHlwIjogIkpXVCJ9.eyJ0aW1lc3RhbXAiOiA...`

### 🚨 Confirmed Violations (1)
| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 43 |

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 43
if ($token == md5(str_rot13("success"))) {
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

#### [SAST-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001) — Dangerous code execution function detected
```js
// Line 1
var a=['fromCharCode','toString','replace','BeJ','\x5cw+','Lyg','SuR','(w(){\x273M\x203L\x27;q\x201l=\x273K\x203I\x203J\x20T\x27;q\x201R=1c\x202I===\x271n\x27;q\x20Y=1R?2I:{};p(Y.3N){1R=1O}q\x202L=...
```
**Suggested fix:**
```js
# Suggested fix: avoid eval/exec; use subprocess with a list (no shell=True)
import subprocess
# WRONG:  subprocess.call('ls ' + user_input, shell=True)
result = subprocess.run(['ls', user_input], capture_output=True, text=True)  # safe
# For dynamic config: use ast.literal_eval() or json.loads() instead of eval()
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: This is the primary defense against code-level flaws. It directly blocks the introduction of vulnerabilities like those found in penetration tests (e.g., Insecure File Upload, IDOR logic). It shifts discovery from a late-stage pen test to the d
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remediate the flagged vulnerability (e.g., use a parameterized query instead of string concatenation) to meet the quality gate standards.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001)

| Sev | Mandard | Title | Line |

| 🟠 HIGH | [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) | Encryption at Rest | 18 |

#### [CRYPT-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001) — Weak hash algorithm MD5 detected
```php
// Line 18
document.getElementById("token").value = md5(rot13(phrase));
```
**Suggested fix:**
```php
# Suggested fix: replace MD5 with SHA-256 (or bcrypt/argon2 for passwords)
import hashlib
digest = hashlib.sha256(data.encode()).hexdigest()   # Python
// const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data).digest('hex');  // Node.js
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: Unencrypted data at rest is vulnerable to theft if storage is compromised, breached, or mishandled. Encryption ensures confidentiality even if physical access is gained.
> 🔧 **Guidance**: Configure encryption for all databases, file systems, and backups. Use AES-256 or better. Store encryption keys in a separate key management system.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=CRYPT-M-001)

#### [SAST-M-001](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001) — Dangerous code execution function detected
```js
// Line 83
var crypto = eval("require('crypto')");
// Line 84
var Buffer = eval("require('buffer').Buffer");
```
**Suggested fix:**
```js
# Suggested fix: avoid eval/exec; use subprocess with a list (no shell=True)
import subprocess
# WRONG:  subprocess.call('ls ' + user_input, shell=True)
result = subprocess.run(['ls', user_input], capture_output=True, text=True)  # safe
# For dynamic config: use ast.literal_eval() or json.loads() instead of eval()
```
> 💡 **Why**: Risk: This is the primary defense against code-level flaws. It directly blocks the introduction of vulnerabilities like those found in penetration tests (e.g., Insecure File Upload, IDOR logic). It shifts discovery from a late-stage pen test to the d
> 🔧 **Guidance**: A developer must remediate the flagged vulnerability (e.g., use a parameterized query instead of string concatenation) to meet the quality gate standards.
> [Full remediation →](https://verify.example-org.internal/?id=SAST-M-001)
